Abstract Multi-teacher Adaptive On-policy Self-Distillation selects a preferred teacher for each token during training, improving mathematical reasoning performance over fixed-teacher methods.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small On-policy Self-distillation (OPSD) has proven effective for post-training large language models by constructing a teacher conditioned on privileged references. However, existing approaches keep the teacher fixed either throughout training by consistently exposing it to the full reference, or within each response by adaptively varying partial reference exposure only across responses. In this paper, we argue that neither of them is sufficient. Instead, every token needs its own teacher, as the teacher preference can vary token by token within the same response. Through a teacher-intervention pilot study, we validate that the teacher preference differs across tokens, and reveal that a preferred teacher can be identified for each token through majority voting and KL-based selection. Motivated by these findings, we propose Multi-teacher Adaptive On-policy Self-Distillation (MTA-OPSD), the first token-level adaptive OPSD method. Our method constructs a multi-teacher pool consisting of candidate teachers, and enables every token to adaptively select its preferred teacher during training. Experiments with Qwen3 models on mathematical reasoning tasks show that MTA-OPSD consistently outperforms existing OPSD approaches with an average gain of up to +5.9 over the base model, highlighting the importance of adapting teacher supervision at the token level. The code is available at https://github.com/xingzhejun/MTA-OPSD.
[04.11.2026 12:51] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает метод Multi-teacher Adaptive On-policy Self-Distillation (MTA-OPSD) для дообучения LLM на задачах математического рассуждения. В отличие от существующих подходов с фиксированным учителем, здесь каждый токен получает собственного учителя, которого выбирают через majority voting и KL-based selection из пула кандидатов. Авторы показывают, что предпочтения учителя действительно различаются от токена к токену, даже внутри одного ответа. Эксперименты с моделями Qwen3 demonstrate прирост качества до +5.9 по сравнению с базовой моделью и превосходство над другими OPSD-методами.", "emoji": "🎯", "title": "Свой учитель для каждого токена: точнее дистилляция в LLM"}

Abstract RoboTryOn translates human demonstration videos into robot videos to train control policies without real robot demonstrations, improving success rates and reducing data collection costs.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Robot foundation models have shown remarkable performance in robot control, yet adapting them to a new task still relies heavily on teleoperated demonstrations. Egocentric human videos offer a scalable substitute, but overlaying rendered robots on human videos or retargeting human poses into robot actions leaves significant visual or action gaps for high-DoF robot embodiments. To address this limitation, we propose RoboTryOn, which treats human-to-robot translation as virtual try-on: try-on keeps the person and swaps the garment, RoboTryOn keeps the demonstrated motion and swaps the human for the robot. Specifically, RoboTryOn converts a human video into a video of the target robot specified by a reference image, while preserving the original task, objects, and background. To maintain visual consistency, we translate selected keyframes autoregressively and use them to guide video synthesis with motion conditioned on the human hand skeleton. Additionally, we use an inverse dynamics model (IDM) that incorporates depth features to infer action labels from synthesized videos for policy learning. Our experiments across multiple humanoid embodiments show that policies trained with our synthetic data improve the average success rate on novel tasks, including challenging bimanual manipulation, from 2% to 60%, without any real robot demonstration of those tasks. Moreover, training with our synthetic data matches the performance of policies trained solely on teleoperated demonstrations at approximately a third of the collection cost, demonstrating the cost efficiency of our approach.
[04.11.2026 12:51] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет RoboTryOn — метод, который переводит видео с демонстрацией задачи человеком в видео с роботом, чтобы обучать политики управления без реальных демонстраций от робота. Подход рассматривает перенос человека в робота как виртуальную примерку: сохраняется движение, объекты и фон, а человек заменяется на целевого робота, заданного референсным изображением. Ключевые кадры переводятся авторегрессивно и используются для генерации видео с учетом скелета кисти, а inverse dynamics model с признаками глубины восстанавливает action labels для обучения policy. Эксперименты на нескольких гуманоидных платформах показывают рост success rate на новых задачах, включая двуручные манипуляции, с 2% до 60%, при стоимости сбора данных примерно в три раза ниже, чем у teleoperation.", "emoji": "🤖", "title": "Робот примеряет человеческие движения: учим политики без телеуправления"}

"Abstract RoboTryOn translates human demonstration videos into robot videos to train control policies without real robot demonstrations, improving success rates and reducing data collection costs.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Robot foundation models have shown remarkable performance in robot control, yet adapting them to a new task still relies heavily on teleoperated demonstrations. Egocentric human videos offer a scalable substitute, but overlaying rendered robots on human videos or retargeting human poses into robot actions leaves significant visual or action gaps for high-DoF robot embodiments. To address this limitation, we propose RoboTryOn, which treats human-to-robot translation as virtual try-on: try-on keeps the person and swaps the garment, RoboTryOn keeps the demonstrated motion and swaps the human for the robot. Abstract Kev-Bio is a vision-language model that guides ultrasound landmark detection via clinical questions, generating spatial probability maps to derive measurements with reduced operator variability.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small **Purpose:** Ultrasound biometry is widely used across various clinical applications, including prenatal and cardiac examinations, to obtain quantitative measurements by placing calipers at anatomical landmarks. However, these measurements remain subject to interoperator variability as they rely largely on manual caliper placement. Conventional methods often use specialized prediction heads for different ultrasound tasks, whereas vision-language models offer flexibility through text instructions. However, direct coordinate prediction does not explicitly model spatial likelihood distributions, limiting the representation of localization ambiguity.   **Methods:** We propose Kev-Bio, a vision-language model that uses clinical measurement questions to guide ultrasound landmark detection. Inspired by System One decision models, Kev-Bio uses decision representations of the language model to generate queries for each caliper endpoint. These queries are matched against shared dense ultrasound features to produce spatial probability maps, from which landmark coordinates and clinical measurements are derived. The model is trained end-to-end using both landmark localization and clinical measurement losses.   **Results:** On the nine-view FU/GU Biometry 2026 dataset, Kev-Bio achieves a mean radial error (MRE) of 22.50 pixels and a clinical measurement mean absolute error (MAE) of 15.38, outperforming the compared baselines in overall performance.   **Conclusion:** These results demonstrate the effectiveness of Kev-Bio for ultrasound landmark detection and clinical measurement across different anatomical views. Our code and models are available at https://github.com/moeinheidari7829/Kev-Bio.

Модель формирует spatial probability maps для каждой точки измерения, из которых вычисляются координаты landmarks и клинические показатели. Обучение проходит end-to-end с использованием функций потерь для локализации и для измерений. Paper text to classify:

"Abstract Kev-Bio is a vision-language model that guides ultrasound landmark detection via clinical questions, generating spatial probability maps to derive measurements with reduced operator variability.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small **Purpose:** Ultrasound biometry is widely used across various clinical applications, including prenatal and cardiac examinations, to obtain quantitative measurements by placing calipers at anatomical landmarks. However, these measurements remain subject to interoperator variability as they rely largely on manual caliper placement. Conventional methods often use specialized prediction heads for different ultrasound tasks, whereas vision-language models offer flexibility through text instructions. However, direct coordinate prediction does not explicitly model spatial likelihood distributions, limiting the representation of localization ambiguity.   **Methods:** We propose Kev-Bio, a vision-language model that uses clinical measurement questions to guide ultrasound landmark detection. Inspired by System One decision models, Kev-Bio uses decision representations of the language model to generate queries for each caliper endpoint. These queries are matched against shared dense ultrasound features to produce spatial probability maps, from which landmark coordinates and clinical measurements are derived. The model is trained end-to-end using both landmark localization and clinical measurement losses.   **Results:** On the nine-view FU/GU Biometry 2026 dataset, Kev-Bio achieves a mean radial error (MRE) of 22.50 pixels and a clinical measurement mean absolute error (MAE) of 15.38, outperforming the compared baselines in overall performance.   **Conclusion:** These results demonstrate the effectiveness of Kev-Bio for ultrasound landmark detection and clinical measurement across different anatomical views. Our code and models are available at https://github.com/moeinheidari7829/Kev-Bio."

Abstract Typed decision models classify source-conflict types faster and cheaper than frontier LLMs, with cascades matching top accuracy at lower cost, though they vary in sensitivity and false-source detection.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small When the sources retrieved for a query disagree, the right response depends on why they disagree: a question with several valid answers, a contested topic, stale information and a false source each call for different behaviour. Cattan et al. (2025) showed that telling a generator the conflict type improves its responses by about 24 points, but that predicting the type is hard even for frontier LLMs. We study whether typed decision models, which return probabilities over fixed options in a single forward pass instead of generating text, can do this classification fast and cheaply. Using the CONFLICTS benchmark and the original paper's prompt verbatim, we evaluate all 11 decision models served by one commercial inference gateway, from 7 providers, against Gemini models on identical inputs. Asked directly, the decision models reach 50.0--67.0% accuracy; 10 of the 11 match or exceed Gemini 2.5 Flash, the paper's model (61.3%), at 4--39x lower billed cost (2 were free) and 2.6--6.0x lower latency. The current Gemini 3.8 Flash (70.0%) is significantly more accurate than 8 of them, but sending each decision model's least confident quarter of queries to it gives a cascade statistically tied with it for 8 of the 11, at 25--37% of its cost. End to end, answers generated with the types of 10 of the 11 follow the expected behaviour as often as with the LLM's types. Decomposing the taxonomy into yes/no questions lowers accuracy for every model, and a repair fitted on one model does not transfer. Pre-specified diagnostics show that dates play no measurable role and that sensitivity to source order varies widely between models. Decision models detect a planted false source less reliably than the LLM as hard labels, but their probabilities still separate planted from clean sources. We release code, prompts and all model outputs at https://github.com/Parv17k/decision-models-conflict-typing.

Модели возвращают вероятности по фиксированным классам за один forward pass, что делает классификацию быстрее и дешевле. На бенчмарке CONFLICTS большинство из 11 моделей не уступает Gemini 2.5 Flash по точности, а каскад с Gemini 3.8 Flash даёт сопоставимое качество при 25–37% стоимости. При этом модели хуже обнаруживают ложные источники, но их вероятности всё же различают подложные и чистые источники.", "emoji": "⚖️", "title": "Быстрая и дешёвая классификация конфликтов источников вместо дорогих LLM"}

Abstract DEPTH generates complex terminal tasks by measuring sequential dependency depth rather than turn count, producing harder benchmarks that improve agent fine-tuning.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Long-horizon terminal tasks are used to evaluate and train agents. Human-curated datasets are scarce, so many synthesis methods have emerged. They usually measure the length of a task by the turns a model needs to pass it, and its difficulty by labels the LLM sets during generation. We find, however, that the turn count largely reflects the habits of the model, not only the demands of the task, and that the labels barely track how often models pass. We describe a terminal task by its work, the number of operations it requires, and its depth, the number of turns that must happen in sequence because the commands of each turn can only be chosen after reading the output of the previous one. Estimating depth as the smallest turn cap, stated to the agent, under which the task passes, we audit 734 tasks from six existing datasets. Synthesis has mostly added work, not depth: 94% of the solved synthetic tasks pass within three turns, yet depth, unlike the turn count, predicts difficulty beyond work. We propose DEPTH, in which a generation agent writes each task one segment at a time in a real software environment, carrying out each segment before writing it and starting the next from the state it leaves. Our 105 tasks exceed every audited dataset in depth and work, and none of six current models passes half of them. Fine-tuning Qwen3-8B on runs from these tasks improves it on two terminal benchmarks.
[04.11.2026 12:51] Response: {"desc": "Авторы предлагают метрику глубины (depth) для терминальных задач, которая измеряет число последовательных шагов, зависящих от вывода предыдущих команд, вместо простого подсчёта ходов агента. Анализ 734 задач из шести датасетов показывает, что число ходов отражает скорее привычки модели, а не сложность задачи, а метки сложности, заданные LLM, слабо коррелируют с реальной успешностью. Предложенный метод DEPTH генерирует задачи по сегментам в реальной программной среде, выполняя каждый сегмент до написания следующего, и даёт набор из 105 задач, более сложный по глубине и объёму работы. Дообучение Qwen3-8B на траекториях, полученных из этих задач, улучшает результаты на двух терминальных бенчмарках.", "emoji": "🧭", "title": "Сложность задачи определяется глубиной последовательных зависимостей, а не числом ходов"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract DEPTH generates complex terminal tasks by measuring sequential dependency depth rather than turn count, producing harder benchmarks that improve agent fine-tuning.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Long-horizon terminal tasks are used to evaluate and train agents. Human-curated datasets are scarce, so many synthesis methods have emerged. They usually measure the length of a task by the turns a model needs to pass it, and its difficulty by labels the LLM sets during generation. We find, however, that the turn count largely reflects the habits of the model, not only the demands of the task, and that the labels barely track how often models pass. We describe a terminal task by its work, the number of operations it requires, and its depth, the number of turns that must happen in sequence because the commands of each turn can only be chosen after reading the output of the previous one. Estimating depth as the smallest turn cap, stated to the agent, under which the task passes, we audit 734 tasks from six existing datasets. Synthesis has mostly added work, not depth: 94% of the solved synthetic tasks pass within three turns, yet depth, unlike the turn count, predicts difficulty beyond work. We propose DEPTH, in which a generation agent writes each task one segment at a time in a real software environment, carrying out each segment before writing it and starting the next from the state it leaves. Our 105 tasks exceed every audited dataset in depth and work, and none of six current models passes half of them. Fine-tuning Qwen3-8B on runs from these tasks improves it on two terminal benchmarks."

Abstract Comparing replacement operators for pruned mixture-of-experts experts reveals that gate scaling and calibration domain strongly affect performance, with a proposed tilted novice operator improving results without dominating all settings.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Training-free expert pruning makes two decisions: which experts to remove, and what the layer computes when its router still selects a removed expert. Published methods change both at once. Holding the mask fixed, we compare replacement operators from three families on OLMoE, DeepSeek-V2-Lite and Moonlight, with Granite as a fourth router, using paired, multiplicity-corrected tests. The largest effect is a choice usually left implicit: the survivors' gate scale. On routers trained without top-k renormalization, renormalizing the survivors' gates, a common implementation choice, costs 0.8-8.5 points and reverses whether rerouting beats MoNE's mean-output replacement; on two routers trained with renormalization, the trained scale wins at 50% pruning. We introduce the tilted novice, a closed-form low-rank map of the pruned expert's input whose rank-0 case is MoNE's constant. At 50% pruning it adds 1.2-1.8% parameters and gains 3.45 and 1.37 points over the constant on OLMoE and Moonlight, stable across calibration draws and largely unchanged by 4- and 3-bit quantization. No operator dominates: trained-scale rerouting is best on DeepSeek-V2-Lite, and a partial copy of each pruned expert is statistically indistinguishable from the tilted novice on two models. Evaluation can change conclusions: at 25% pruning MoNE's constant keeps 92-97% of dense multiple-choice accuracy but only 5-65% of GSM8K and 0-17% of code scores, and the calibration domain moves the mask 4-9 times more than resampling. We propose a five-item reporting checklist.

Ключевой эффект связан с масштабом gate-весов выживших экспертов: их ренормализация без top-k renormalization ухудшает качество, тогда как обученный масштаб лучше при 50% прунинге. Авторы предлагают tilted novice, низкоранговое отображение входа удалённого эксперта, которое обобщает константу MoNE (rank-0) и даёт прирост на OLMoE и Moonlight при +1.2-1.8% параметров. Итог: ни один оператор не доминирует везде, а результаты сильно зависят от калибровочного домена и выбора метрик, поэтому авторы предлагают чек-лист из пяти пунктов для отчётности.", "emoji": "🧩", "title": "Масштаб gate-весов и калибровка решают судьбу прунинга экспертов в MoE"}

Abstract Cross-pathway concept unlearning in unified multimodal models is addressed by a new benchmark and method that jointly suppresses target concept generation and recognition while preserving other capabilities.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Unified multimodal models (UMMs) integrate image generation and visual understanding, but are existing single-pathway unlearning methods sufficient to suppress target concepts across both? In this work, we study cross-pathway concept unlearning. Our goal is to prevent a model from both generating images of a target concept and identifying it in input images, while preserving other capabilities. To this end, we introduce UUBench, a diagnostic benchmark covering 30 target concepts and 60 semantically related neighboring concepts. Experiments on representative UMMs show that the evaluated single-pathway methods achieve limited joint forgetting: generation-side editors strongly suppress target generation but yield limited forgetting in visual understanding, while understanding-side baselines struggle to achieve substantial forgetting even in their own pathway. Their direct composition also yields limited joint forgetting. We therefore propose UniForget, achieving joint forgetting rates of 80.0% on BAGEL-7B-MoT and 92.7% on Janus-Pro-7B, outperforming representative baselines. Further analysis reveals trade-offs between joint forgetting and capability preservation.
[04.11.2026 12:51] Response: {"desc": "Статья рассматривает проблему cross-pathway concept unlearning в унифицированных мультимодальных моделях (UMM), которые одновременно генерируют изображения и понимают визуальный контекст. Авторы вводят бенчмарк UUBench с 30 целевыми концептами и 60 семантически близкими соседними концептами для диагностики забывания. Показано, что существующие методы unlearning, применяемые только к одному пути (генерации или пониманию), плохо справляются с совместным забыванием, а их простая композиция тоже недостаточна. Experiments on representative UMMs show that the evaluated single-pathway methods achieve limited joint forgetting: generation-side editors strongly suppress target generation but yield limited forgetting in visual understanding, while understanding-side baselines struggle to achieve substantial forgetting even in their own pathway. Their direct composition also yields limited joint forgetting. We therefore propose UniForget, achieving joint forgetting rates of 80.0% on BAGEL-7B-MoT and 92.7% on Janus-Pro-7B, outperforming representative baselines. Further analysis reveals trade-offs between joint forgetting and capability preservation."

Abstract Using Muon to train mixture-of-experts language models increases routing imbalance in early layers, which can be mitigated by centering router inputs or reducing the optimizer learning rate.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Many open mixture-of-experts (MoE) language models train with Muon instead of AdamW. Most balance the load with a bias on each expert's routing score, adjusted after every step. Their recipes disagree on whether Muon should also train the router. In models with 55M and 108M active parameters, we measure how the optimizer changes the span of these biases, the largest minus the smallest in a layer. The span grows when all tokens favor the same experts, so it shows how hard a layer is to balance. At learning rates tuned on shorter runs, Muon raised the span of the first MoE layer at every seed, roughly doubling it on average, while the other layers changed much less. The rise came from Muon on the rest of the network, whichever optimizer trained the router. In those runs, the inputs to the first router shared a direction, and soon after the warmup its busiest expert averaged four to seven times its fair share. Over the first 3,000 steps, subtracting the batch mean from these inputs removed this overload while barely changing the loss. On the smaller model, halving Muon's learning rate removed most of the rise and slightly improved the loss. Moving only the router to AdamW made the loads shift more during the warmup and slightly raised the loss, so at this scale the results favor keeping the router on Muon.

Авторы измеряют разброс смещений роутера (router bias) по слоям и обнаруживают, что Muon усиливает дисбаланс в первом MoE-слое, где входы роутера оказываются сонаправленными. Abstract DeepMem is a causal multiscale byte-level architecture that uses invertible subband transforms and fast weight memory to compress sequences for efficient reasoning while preserving exact byte identity via lossless carriers and attention-based super-resolution decoding.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Byte level language models avoid tokenizer dependence and provide universal coverage, but processing every byte at full resolution is expensive. We introduce DeepMem, a causal multiscale architecture that separates semantic compression from exact byte preservation. A learned invertible subband transform constructs a hierarchy of shorter sequences, while a lossless carrier and explicit detail sidebands preserve byte identity. Fast weight memory blocks operate along the encoder, with iterative shared state computation at the compressed bottleneck, while an attention based super resolution decoder restores byte resolution representations. DeepMem achieves stronger language modeling performance than a matched byte level Transformer on FineWeb-Edu and PG19, and obtains the best macro average performance across the evaluated FLORES languages. It also improves PIQA accuracy while remaining competitive on code modeling, multiple choice reasoning, and byte level corruption robustness. Bottleneck refinement requires approximately 7{,}600times fewer counted FLOPs than equivalent full resolution reasoning, while maintaining competitive evaluation throughput.

Обучаемое обратимое subband-преобразование строит иерархию более коротких последовательностей, а lossless carrier и детальные sideband-каналы сохраняют идентичность каждого байта. "Abstract DeepMem is a causal multiscale byte-level architecture that uses invertible subband transforms and fast weight memory to compress sequences for efficient reasoning while preserving exact byte identity via lossless carriers and attention-based super-resolution decoding.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Byte level language models avoid tokenizer dependence and provide universal coverage, but processing every byte at full resolution is expensive. Abstract Kierston and KierstonBench provide a simulated city-scale environment to evaluate large language models on criminal investigation tasks using linked surveillance records, revealing that only frontier models perform reliably while remaining vulnerable to deception.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Large language models are candidates for collecting and analyzing records in criminal investigations and mass surveillance. Their capability and accuracy in this work cannot currently be measured. Real deployments lack the ground truth needed to score a model's conclusions, and no benchmark evaluates these capabilities in a simulated environment. We present Kierston, a city-scale simulation engine, and KierstonBench, a benchmark that tests these capabilities against known simulated events. Kierston models approximately 125,000 residents of Allentown, Pennsylvania, generating their daily activities and the telecommunications, financial, administrative and license-plate records that surveillance and investigative systems collect about them. These records derive from a common behavioral timeline and are distributed across sources representing separate providers. Models investigate through a restricted records interface, linking observations across sources without access to the underlying simulation. The benchmark comprises 32 task families covering record linkage, routine reconstruction, incident investigation and assessment of deceptive accounts. In an evaluation of sixteen language models, most could not perform records investigation and submitted identifications the records did not support. A small number of frontier models solved most of the tasks and abstained when evidence was insufficient. In some cases these models still fell for common deceptions and assumptions, and none replaced an investigator.

Abstract GigaSpeech 3 provides 39,000 hours of weakly transcribed dialectal Arabic speech, enabling improved recognition across five dialects via self-supervised labeling and noisy-student refinement.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Dialectal Arabic speech recognition remains constrained by the scarcity of dialect-resolved transcribed speech despite the abundance of web audio. We introduce GigaSpeech 3, a corpus of 39,000 hours and 10 million weakly transcribed speech segments covering Algerian, Egyptian, Iraqi, Saudi, and Syrian Arabic. Starting from 60 hours of manual transcription per dialect, we train labeling models using speech-recognition-biased self-supervised learning and apply them to dialect-matched web audio. Data selection and four rounds of noisy-student refinement for Saudi and Syrian Arabic yield the final corpus. We validate its utility through dialect-specific and multi-dialect speech recognition training and cross-domain evaluation. On GigaSpeechBench, the final dialect-specific models outperform their labeling models across all five dialects and achieve a macro-average word error rate of 23.86%, a 16.6% relative reduction from the strongest third-party system covering all five dialects, while outperforming the best third-party result on three dialects. A multi-dialect model using self-predicted dialect identities achieves a macro-average word error rate of 25.51% while also supporting Modern Standard Arabic. Across eight conditions from three external corpora, a GigaSpeech 3-trained Zipformer achieves the lowest word error rate among six evaluated models in every condition, while adapting the Qwen3 speech recognizer on GigaSpeech 3 improves performance across all eight conditions. We release the corpus and recipes as a foundation for dialect-aware Arabic speech processing.

Разметка выполняется моделями, обученными через self-supervised learning с учётом распознавания речи, а затем данные уточняются методом noisy-student refinement. Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Dialectal Arabic speech recognition remains constrained by the scarcity of dialect-resolved transcribed speech despite the abundance of web audio. We introduce GigaSpeech 3, a corpus of 39,000 hours and 10 million weakly transcribed speech segments covering Algerian, Egyptian, Iraqi, Saudi, and Syrian Arabic. Starting from 60 hours of manual transcription per dialect, we train labeling models using speech-recognition-biased self-supervised learning and apply them to dialect-matched web audio. Data selection and four rounds of noisy-student refinement for Saudi and Syrian Arabic yield the final corpus. We validate its utility through dialect-specific and multi-dialect speech recognition training and cross-domain evaluation. On GigaSpeechBench, the final dialect-specific models outperform their labeling models across all five dialects and achieve a macro-average word error rate of 23.86%, a 16.6% relative reduction from the strongest third-party system covering all five dialects, while outperforming the best third-party result on three dialects. A multi-dialect model using self-predicted dialect identities achieves a macro-average word error rate of 25.51% while also supporting Modern Standard Arabic. Across eight conditions from three external corpora, a GigaSpeech 3-trained Zipformer achieves the lowest word error rate among six evaluated models in every condition, while adapting the Qwen3 speech recognizer on GigaSpeech 3 improves performance across all eight conditions. We release the corpus and recipes as a foundation for dialect-aware Arabic speech processing."

Abstract An autonomous agent adaptively designs target-specific multiple sequence alignments for AlphaFold 3 by reasoning over biological hypotheses and self-directed experimentation, improving protein interface prediction with fewer sequences.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Protein-complex structure prediction with AlphaFold 3 depends on MSA content, depth, and cross-chain pairing. Current pipelines apply uniform recipes, typically maximizing depth and pairing by species, yet even whether cross-chain pairing helps AF3 remains contested. We show that the value of both depth and pairing is strongly target-dependent, so no single recipe is optimal across interfaces. We introduce an autonomous agent that treats a frozen AF3 as a black-box experimental instrument. For each target, the agent formulates biological hypotheses, analyzes and reorganizes the MSA, and iteratively refines sequence selection and pairing using predictor label-free signals alone. On 1{,}015 protein--protein interfaces from three benchmarks released after AF3's training cutoff, the agent improves overall interface DockQ over AF3's default pipeline and fixed MSA recipes on every benchmark, while using on average 60\% fewer MSA sequences than the default. Gains are largest for inter-species heteromers, where species-based pairing is unreliable: mean DockQ increases from 0.465 to 0.612 on FoldBench and from 0.482 to 0.562 on HD439. The improvements arise from both sequence construction and pairing policy, including deliberate non-pairing.These results recast MSA design as a target-specific problem and show that an autonomous agent can adapt it at test time through biological reasoning and self-directed, confidence-guided experimentation.

Авторы представляют автономного агента, который рассматривает замороженную модель AF3 как «чёрный ящик», формулирует биологические гипотезы и итеративно отбирает последовательности и правила спаривания, опираясь только на label-free сигналы уверенности предсказания. Эксперименты на 1015 интерфейсах белок-белок показывают рост DockQ относительно стандартного пайплайна при использовании в среднем на 60% меньше последовательностей MSA. "Abstract An autonomous agent adaptively designs target-specific multiple sequence alignments for AlphaFold 3 by reasoning over biological hypotheses and self-directed experimentation, improving protein interface prediction with fewer sequences.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Protein-complex structure prediction with AlphaFold 3 depends on MSA content, depth, and cross-chain pairing. Current pipelines apply uniform recipes, typically maximizing depth and pairing by species, yet even whether cross-chain pairing helps AF3 remains contested. We show that the value of both depth and pairing is strongly target-dependent, so no single recipe is optimal across interfaces. We introduce an autonomous agent that treats a frozen AF3 as a black-box experimental instrument. On 1{,}015 protein--protein interfaces from three benchmarks released after AF3's training cutoff, the agent improves overall interface DockQ over AF3's default pipeline and fixed MSA recipes on every benchmark, while using on average 60\% fewer MSA sequences than the default. The improvements arise from both sequence construction and pairing policy, including deliberate non-pairing.These results recast MSA design as a target-specific problem and show that an autonomous agent can adapt it at test time through biological reasoning and self-directed, confidence-guided experimentation."
